# The one list — every open decision, one register (started 2026-09-17; rows added through 2026-10-01; each row's status column is the owner of its status)

Folded from: the consolidation note (D-A…D-J), the three seat receipts (daemons D1–D4, author
C1 and its three decisions, run §4–§5), scout C (D1–D12), scout D (D-1…D-8), scout E (E-1…E-10
and its eight slices), scout F (F-1…F-12 and the seven squares), the LCNF survey (§7, the LLVM
steer). Where two or more said the same thing under different names, one row; the sources are
listed so the reasoning can be found. **Owner** = a ruling is needed; **do** = no decision
content, scheduled. Recommendations are the coordinator's, after reading all six inputs.

**Reviewed 2026-09-18 against HEAD** (after the census, `fold_of`, the checker as one
`Except`-valued fold, the `explain` and `effTy` callers slices, and the first Schema cuts): a
status column, verified row by row against the tree with file and line; rows 12, 13, 30 (for
`denote` and `effTy`), 36, 37, 38 closed; rows 39 (the Schema wipe as one ruling) and 40 (the
callers phase, continued) added; the order re-cut at the foot. The owner's steer of 2026-09-17
("coherence of the algebra is the metric, not consumers; convert to folds where the shape
allows, delete at a good place") is applied to the rows argued from consumer counts (4, 9, 12).

| 203 | A planned goal is a named theorem whose body is `sorry` | Amends the trust rule "no `sorry`". The ledger's `Obligation` marker could not be used downstream, so every decomposition was a registered conditional theorem matched by the plan. A planned goal is now declared by one command, `proof_goal G : P`, which adds `theorem G : P := sorry` and tags it. Downstream proofs use `G` as a theorem. A theorem's status is read from its dependencies: proved when it reaches no goal, proved modulo the goals the walk stops at otherwise, and open when it is a goal. Guardrails: (1) `sorry` appears only as the whole body of a declared goal; the token stays banned in source, and Lean's sorry warning is off only inside the command. (2) A goal is a theorem of a proposition, in `Effect4.Laws` or a `Test` fixture; the `Effect4` root reaches none, so no compiled code holds a `sorry`. (3) The axiom gate's ceiling stays `[propext, Quot.sound]`; a declaration reaches `sorryAx` only through goals, and the gate reports the declarations that rest on goals. (4) A registry claim or a requirement's top node counts as proved only when it reaches no goal. (5) A goal a counterexample refutes is deleted or restated. The `Obligation` marker, its placeholders, the registered reductions and `#obligation_close` are deleted | the coordinator's proposal in conversation, 2026-10-04; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-04-reference-scout/proof-graph.md`; LeanArchitect and leanblueprint (`vendor/refs/MANIFEST.tsv`) | owner | **ruled 2026-10-04** (owner: "yes lets go ahead with that") |

## Derived forms, queues and the shared foundations (2026-10-05)

Ten groups of rulings, all of 2026-10-05.

- **Rows 214 to 218, and row 130's ruling.** The owner ratified the seven recommendations of the
  derived forms plan in session ("all's recommended").
- **Rows 219 to 234.** The owner ratified Codex's recommendations "as recommended" and had Codex
  relay them. The relay is `coordinator-note.txt` in
  `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet`. The same day the owner said that Codex's
  relays count as coming from the owner, and then gave the word in session to land them.
- **Rows 235 to 237.** The owner answered the coordinator's five follow-up questions in session
  ("yes to all five, go with your recommendations").
- **Rows 238 and 239, and row 227's amendment.** The owner signed off the waiting design's three
  points in session ("yes to all three"), after Codex's review of that note.
- **Rows 240 to 243.** The owner ratified the Queue contract's three choices and its refused
  operations in session ("go ahead with all three", "ratify as recommended").
- **Rows 244 to 248.** The owner ratified the mask's three directions in session ("yes to all
  three"), after Codex's review of the mask's second note. The same message corrected the
  coordinator's reading of "the TypeScript package" (row 247). It also ruled the migration to
  4.0.1 in increments (row 248).
- **Rows 249 to 252.** The owner answered the coordinator's four open points in session: keep
  `void` at `Scope.close`, download the driver, land Codex's follow-ups, and the order after
  the fold.
- **Row 253.** The owner spoke on the migration in session, and told Codex the same day that
  its mechanics are fine. Codex relayed that phrase.
- **Row 254.** The owner spoke on dogfooding in session, after Codex's review of the acceptance
  programs.
- **Row 255.** The owner ratified the five proposals of the Queue's steps design in session
  ("yes, go with your recommendations on the five proposals"), after Codex's two reviews of
  that note. The same hour the owner allowed its seat as a third one (row 237's amendment).

The evidence behind rows 219 to 234 is finite runs, finite models, source reading and primary
literature. None of it is a Lean proof. Each row's obligations are open parts of the requirement
rows in the semantics registry (`tools/ProofGraph/Registry.lean`), and
`git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/foundation-contracts.md` consolidates them in one table.

| # | decision | recommendation | sources | who | status (dated per row) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 214 | What a derived form is | A derived form is a row of the form table: an expansion template with a typing lemma, a generated builder, a readable expansion and one behaviour law (DI-89). The template alphabet grows to spell `catchIf`, `select`, `iterate` and atom application. A helper written for one program stays an authoring function, as p1's `retryForm` is | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-04-claude-lead/derived-forms-plan.md` §3, question 1; DI-89 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; not landed |

| 219 | The Queue's order, and where a message is consumed | Takers are served in strict request order, and a request that never waits follows the same order. A message leaves the buffer only in the atomic step of the taker that receives it. A signal invites another attempt and reserves nothing. A batch at the head can block smaller requests: a signed difference from 4.0.1, where a later taker can receive a message while an earlier one waits (probe P1). The time of a retry stays observable in a batch's length, a sliding queue's value and a dropping offer's answer. The contract follows the release where the pin is defective (probes P2, P3 and P4). The first profile requires a positive capacity. Rendezvous at capacity zero needs its own two-party commit and withdrawal rules, and `sliding` at capacity zero is refused. Rejected: a reservation that a withdrawal returns (Codex's countermodels); the `readyFirst` order | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/queues-review.md` F2, F5, F6 and proposals 2 to 4; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/queue-probes/QueueModel.lean`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/contracts-and-literature.md`; DI-11 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; not landed. The same day rows 241 and 242 apply it to `dropping` at capacity zero and to `poll` and `clear` |
| 220 | How the Queue's signal is delivered | Posted, for the default Effect 4 profile: the signalled request runs its step when the dispatcher runs the posted task, as rc.112 and 4.0.1 do (probes P5 to P7). An ordinary `Deferred` stays inline. Each producer of a posted wake keeps its own rules explicit: which waiters it selects and when (a live selection or a captured one), its priority, and its coalescing. The Queue takes no dependency on Latch, and row 81's Latch obligations stay open. An inline Queue delivery stays a possible profile that is explicitly different; it gives Effect 3's answers on P5 to P7, and it was the coordinator's lean | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/queues-review.md` F2, F5.9 and proposal 10; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/review.md`; row 81 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**: posted; not landed. Row 238 gives the construct, and row 240 extends it to the offerer's signal |
| 221 | The shared waiting wrapper | One attempt, registration and retry infrastructure is shared. Each module owns its enrolment and its cancellation. A body is admitted by a checked body profile that tracks every dynamic cell access; "any body" is withdrawn. The decision to wait and the registration are one admitted transition. A wake invites another attempt and reserves no result. The invariant says where an outstanding notification lives at every transition: store debt, queued commands, dispatcher work, or the receiver's accepted continuation. The logical request, the await token and the wake batch's phase are three different things. Acceptance traces, each with its positive control: a notification before the await; a cancellation after the selection and before the delivery; a late delivery with an old token after rearming | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/transactions-and-clock.md` F5 and proposal 1; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/tx-probes/TxModel.lean`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/foundation-audit.md` §2 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; its design is owed before the first Queue slice (row 233) |
| 222 | Cancellation of an operation that waits | When cancellation wins before consumption, the request is withdrawn and nothing is consumed. After consumption the state stays committed, even when the caller is interrupted before its continuation receives the value. The observation records four things apart: the commitment, the operation's exit, the entry of the caller's continuation, and the fiber's exit. No law says that an interrupted take consumed nothing, or that every committed result reaches the caller's code; masking gives no such guarantee. An acquisition whose lifetime must be protected, such as a permit, keeps that lifetime inside a bracketed operation with installed cleanup. Evidence: a protected `Queue.take` consumes and reaches its result, and the restored mask interrupts before the caller continues, on rc.112, 4.0.1 and Effect 3.22.2 (five controls on each) | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/atomic/cancel-return/README.md`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/queue-probes/cancel-return.ts` and its three outputs; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/literature/tasks/contracts.md` | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; not landed |
| 223 | The atomic body (the first profile of row 80) | An atomic body is a restricted fragment of `Eff`: pure terms, transactional reads and writes of existing cells, success, failure, retry, and flat composition. The first profile excludes allocation, host effects, recovery from a failure inside the body, general loops, and calls that are unresolved or reentrant. Nesting is flat: only the outer attempt commits or discards. The commit's bookkeeping, the notifications included, finishes before any receiver's continuation runs; a body with an inline delivery is not one region. Reads and writes are recorded in their dynamic order, and no static overestimate stands in where the wake behaviour is claimed. Transition bodies are shared across modules. Fair composition of tickets that are enrolled apart is outside the first profile (the opposing-ticket cycle). Evidence: under `uninterruptible` and `PreventSchedulerYield`, a waiter of a completed `Deferred` still runs inline and sees the middle state, on rc.112 and 4.0.1 | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/review.md` §B and §D; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/foundation-audit.md` §3; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-09-19-stm-scout.md`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/tx-probes/TxModel.lean` (`opposingTickets`); rows 80 and 84 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; its design is owed before the first consumer; row 80's version erasure and target agreement stay open |
| 224 | Alternatives inside a transaction | A fallback on retry only, the rule of Harris et al.: the left branch's writes are discarded, the enclosing writes are kept, and when both branches retry the wait covers both branches' dependencies. A failure propagates. This is the project's contract. It is not Effect 3's `STM.orElse`, which also catches a failure and drops the left reads, nor `STM.orTry`, which keeps the left writes (Codex's runs on 3.22.2). Effect 4 has no alternative. It lands after the first transaction path, with a take from either of two queues as its consumer | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/literature/stm/contract-findings.md`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/scout/alternatives-v3.out`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/tx-probes/TxModel.lean` (`eitherTrace`) | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; not landed |
| 225 | Posted work is an `Eff` body with task metadata (the wider form of row 81) | A posted task's body is an existing `Eff` body, and no callback language is added. Its metadata stays explicit and separate: the dispatcher's owner, the execution identity, the lifetime's owner, the captures, the service context, the receiver's token, and the completion rule. A posted callback is not replaced by a child fork in general: a deferred fork allocates a supervised child and posts its first evaluation, and the lifetimes differ. The first slice has one posting site, the Queue's, with its typed environment. `TaskMeans` (`src/Effect4/Laws/Machine/Book.lean`), `SnapshotTyped` and `EditTask` (`src/Effect4/Laws/Program/Typed/Assembly.lean`) and the receiver's stale-token guard stay on the integration path. DB-13's rule that a dispatcher serves after its owner's exit stays a premise | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/task/review.md`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/literature/tasks/contracts.md` §2; rows 81 and 82; DB-13 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; its design is owed before the first Queue slice |
| 226 | Work limits inside an owned operation | The first finite atomic bodies run under a proved sufficient embedded budget that covers the registration, the cleanup and the selected delivery; a cut inside the operation is excluded, and it is not called a resumption. Incremental execution keeps a full suspension of the driver: the commands, the remaining dispatcher tasks, the enclosing flush or clock phase, and any atomic owner. Continuing with budgets `n` and `k` equals one uninterrupted run with `n + k` under the same inputs. A fresh command on a changed machine is not a resumption. The suspension's boundary is designed now, and it is implemented before any consumer promises resumable ownership | `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/foundation-audit.md` §1; `docs/core/machine-state.md` §5; row 84 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; the embedded budget's connector and the suspension are both open |
| 227 | The mask that restores (amends rows 208 and 216) | `restore` is a first-order lexical reference to its mask's saved incoming interruptibility. The run resolves the dynamic activation and restores on every exit: success, failure, interruption and nested entry. The first profile refuses a reference that escapes its mask. `restore` is not unconditional interruptible execution: when the caller is already masked it is the identity, as rc.112's `uninterruptibleMask` is. No third masking state is added. Cleanup is installed before an interruption can observe an acquired resource or a committed registration. | 259 | Semaphore's wake is the live scan (under rows 219, 221, 233 and 238) | A release posts one helper, by row 238's construct. The helper visits the waiters one at a time, in the order of enrolment. Before each visit it stops if no permit is free. Otherwise it resolves the hint of the next waiter whose count fits. That waiter resumes inside the helper's task and runs its own take step, which checks the count again. The helper then reads the state again. A wake reserves nothing, and a permit commits in the waiter's own step. | 274 | The fiber numbers of the truth lane's compared fields | The Lean face writes a fiber under its number in the recorder's order of first sight. It does so in every compared field that holds a fiber: each row of the schedule, and the exits of the fork run and of the sync run. Lean's reduction wrote the machine's ids, which follow the allocation order, and its docstring said that the two orders agree. They differ when the recorder sees a fiber late: a scheduled daemon child has a `forked` row on neither face, so a fiber that is forked after it and runs before it takes the smaller number on rc.112. `pQueueOrder` is the first such program. So this is a repair of the Lean face and no new rule. The lane then compares the two faces up to one renaming of the fibers, and it no longer checks the machine's allocation order. | 307 | The owner's rulings of 2026-10-07: the first share, the main draw and the order of work | (1) **The first share has two audiences**: people of Lean and of verification, and Effect developers. (2) **Authoring new programs is the priority, and ingest is not.** The census of real projects stays a parked fact. (3) **The main draw**, in the owner's words. An agent produces the code, "using Lean and our tooling". The others are "running host sessions on verified programs", "verified implementations based on our semantics", "visualizing it, stepping through it" and "operating on the program as data, as first-order data". (4) **The first authoring path is an agent through the data operations**, behind an MCP wrapper. The Lean builders are the second path. The MCP authoring is designed "carefully and slowly so that I understand it". It gets its own design note, and the owner walks that note before a seat implements it. (5) **A host session runs on all three lowered forms "if we can"**: OCaml, Effect and Lean. (6) **The share waits on four gates, and none is skipped**: the claims table with the refusal report, the top gaps closed, the guarantee of a host session, and lowering (row 28). (7) **Foundations come first.** The base features of the language land before a feature that needs them. (8) **The to-do application is the centre of the dogfooding**: SQL, an API that updates to-dos, proved invariants, several programs, host sessions and its use as data. It exercises the whole loop, and it shows the gaps. (9) **The theorems of a program are part of its data, and that API is not improvised.** It rests on the theory of algebraic effects and handlers. The owner agrees its form before a claim is added to the application. (10) **Streams are in the plan, and recursive types are parked** | the owner, in session on 2026-10-07, by voice and by option cards | owner | **ruled 2026-10-07**. Not ruled: where the language stops computing, and whether integers enter now. The coordinator recommends that integers wait |

| 330 | The verified module toolkit: Codex's four answers on the MODULES design | (1) **Steps as data** (question 1): a module's step is first-order data over the checked `Ty` domain, and its field references are data. A Lean structure ties to its `Ty` by deriving (`Modeled`). (2) **Latch's wake** (question 2): match rc.112's batching, one flush helper per batch (`[1,2,9]`, probe MODS-10). Cancellation and reentrant controls land before the component's law. (3) **The clients of a module's law** (question 3): a precisely checked client subset, against an abstract transition model. Interruption and cleanup stay observable. A client that inspects a helper fiber's numeric identity is outside the subset (finding F4). Row 329 stays open, and its questions 2 and 3 take this answer as their recommendation. (4) **Compatibility** (question 4): four separate results. Shared-model conformance: each implementation's behaviours lie inside the model's. Directed compatibility: one implementation's behaviours lie inside the other's, in a named direction, under a named observation and profile. Equal observations: both directions hold, or the finite named clients agree, labelled tested. Signed differences: each accepted unequal observation matches a ruled row and its exact predicted pair. | 332 | The library's shape: entry modules, the rename, and built artifacts | (1) **Entry modules** (representation): a user imports `Effect4.Author` (program and module authoring, `deriving Modeled`, the step language, `derive_step`, `eff_module` and the module form, the wrappers), `Effect4.Run` (runs, tapes, the host session), `Effect4.Emit` (TypeScript, Schema documents, the codec), `Effect4.Library` (the prebuilt composed modules, each also alone) and `Effect4.Laws.Author` (the shared laws, `derive_step_eval`). `#explain` and `#obligations` are their own import, `Effect4.Laws.Author.Explain` (amended 2026-10-09, owner: they read the semantics registry, so a registry edit then rebuilds no author's module). An import gate checks a user's file, in report mode until the acceptance programs move. | 354 | The owner's rulings of 2026-10-11: the design language in documents (the grill after row 353's landing) | (1) **Authority** (representation): the design language becomes one core authority in `docs/core/`, mapped in `AGENTS.md` and reconciled from `git:eb00182dfc0702ac0eefbc83bfa22ca882bfbafd:docs/design/design-language.md` and rows 308, 336, 337 and this row. (2) **Status by shape** (meaning): an evidence status is a mark of the promise-and-keeping family, with no hue: proved filled, modulo half filled, a planned goal hollow, open a dashed outline, refuted the failure cross. A box's growth becomes a scaling and a route's reveal a clip along its direction; a growing edge's tapered tip goes. (5) **Playback** (meaning): a motion is the image of one edit or one machine decision. A document plays a step on the reader's demand, with no script, and shows the frames beside it as a strip of small multiples. Nothing loops, nothing moves between two concurrent events, and a reader who prefers reduced motion sees the end frames. (9) **Organic strokes** (meaning): the default look keeps the forms that carry a meaning (width by share, collars, round corners) and sets the noise to zero; a look may turn the noise on. (10) **Stroke width** (meaning): width carries share; depth moves to tone and to haloed gaps at crossings. (4) **Seams to clean** (domain): the view's tool pages written as strings, the architecture map's CDN scripts and fonts, and the looks' two sources come before the site grows past its first slice. (5) **The C player is first-class** (meaning; the owner's steer the same evening): it plays the same data with the same meaning as the web: the same frames, the same motion, the same timing. The web is one more fold, never the reference. With the motion port (row 360, P5) the stream carries a step's tracks, timing and easing, and `play.c` samples them itself; the kit's easing readings are whole-number arithmetic rounded down once, so a differential check like `v -c` holds C's poses equal to `Track.at` at every moment. Direction, not a slice: Lean lowers the view to C now through its own compiler, and later the view and the player become an Effect program lowered by Effect4's own pipeline. (6) **The report's seam** (representation; proposed by the coordinator the same night, the understanding note's Q6): the semantics report writes its one document as the JSON contract, and the site's assembler reads it back (`Tools.Document.Json.Document.reads`, refused unless the document read writes the same JSON). | 374 | The owner's rulings of 2026-10-13, evening: pegs and strings replace blended joins | (1) **Pegs and strings** (representation): the tube language's geometry is a grid of pegs and taut strings. A peg is a circle on a grid point; a body is one closed string wrapped round pegs in order, each peg wrapped convex (inside the body) or concave (the string passes it outside: a notch, a crotch, a fillet). Between pegs the string runs on their common tangent, round a peg on its circle, so an outline is arcs and lines with no kink by construction, and its casing is the same string round the same pegs one line smaller. The owner's words: "compose and scale on grid with drawing string over pegs system", after a generative-art reference. The kit's notch blends (row 371, S24) and the pen's join and stretch outlines are retired from the pen as the port lands. (2) **A split is a tuning fork** (meaning): the stem enters the back of a U whose halves are the arms; the crotch is a concave peg, a perfect half circle; the stem's neck is a small concave peg on each side; a merge is the fork read the other way ("more like tuning forks with smooth perfect U and splitting semantics"). (3) **A vessel's connector is square and rounded** (meaning): a vessel is a string round its corner pegs, and a pipe enters its end face by a concave peg at each side of the neck ("more square rounded end connectors vs bulbs"). (4) **The interrupt film plays in order** (meaning): the surge is spent where it lands, then the lane draws back, then the stop is set (`7e39d2a62`). (5) **Motion is computed as a light board and drawn as fluid** (representation): the marking of slot pegs changes on the machine's ticks, and every style channel is a value per grid point on aligned rhythms, computed in one pass; the drawing glides units between slots and fuses neighbours into slugs, natural and organic, never a board of bulbs ("I don't want it to look like an actual light board ... natural fluid organic. But using that technique, just for speed and consistent semantics"). (6) **The picture is built into the run's loop** (meaning): each control's event yields its marking change, the board is their fold, and it is exposed in the session's view ("this should all just come from the running of the machine ... built in to the loop"). | 381 | The owner's rulings of 2026-10-14: the bank of verified implementations, its claims, its runtimes, how a program chooses one, and the first release | The owner, 2026-10-14, by voice: a bank of verified implementations that compose, so a queue is never remade when a proof of one exists; Effect's semantics always provided; the same services runnable in Effect TypeScript, under the OCaml driver and from lowered native code. A bank entry is `Effect4.Bank.Entry` (`src/Effect4/Program/Authoring/Claims.lean`): a block, its content digest, its rows, its Lean model and its claim. (1) **What verified certifies** (meaning; the owner's correction later the same day): the entry's logic, a theorem about its behaviour under the semantics, which refines its Lean model. Faithfulness to Effect is the reference model's, checked once against rc.112 by the runtime census and the truth lane, never per entry. Agreement of the implementations (the Lean machine, the OCaml engine, the printed TypeScript, later C) is the language's, proved once (the machine against the meaning, LCNF, the printer's read-back), never per entry. (2) **The scheduler** (meaning; the owner's correction later the same day): it enters only the logic of a concurrent program. Such a claim says what holds in every behaviour the semantics allows, or, through schedule independence (row 361), that they all give one result. A sequential program's claims mention no scheduler. That the runtimes behave alike is (1)'s language-level agreement, not a claim about schedulers. (3) **Services are records of closures, held as data** (representation; the owner's answer later the same day, replacing the link-time choice first carded): faithful to Effect, where a service in the context is a runtime value and the layer provided there, possibly chosen at run time, decides the implementation. A closure is a code reference and captured values: a program path now, a content digest once content addressing lands, one carrier for both. Row 340 (2) is amended for closures that name code; row 118 is answered. A closure never crosses the host boundary. (4) **What ships first** (domain): a TypeScript package of verified Effect modules with Effect's own APIs (Queue, Semaphore, Ref, Deferred, PubSub, Pool, and emitted services), each printed from Lean, checked against rc.112, with its bank claim. Native lowerings come later, behind the same APIs. (5) **Claims are Lean statements from templates** (the owner, later the same day): a claim is a Lean statement over the app's meaning; common shapes are generated as templates to fill (an entry's result, a state cell's invariant, a per-request postcondition, an exactly-once finalizer); agents write Lean directly, and the session tool shows each claim's status. (6) **The runtime anywhere** (domain; the owner's words, later the same day): "the whole point of the ocaml (and wasm) is that we'll have an effect 4 runtime anywhere that can run eff". | 382 | The owner's rulings of 2026-10-14: how verified modules compose | The owner, 2026-10-14: "the challenge is how do we do composition?... we'll grow built in verified implementations like stream etc that have effect APIs but are also implemented with verified lean for lowering too but those apis need to be generic to eventually build any service like that". Today composition exists in code (SynchronizedRef's ops call Semaphore's; the Pool builds on Semaphore) and not in proofs. (1) **A module's claim is contextual refinement of its model** (meaning): every client of the implementation behaves as it would with the model in its place. For clients on many fibers this is linearizability (Filipović, O'Hearn, Rinetzky and Yang, "Abstraction for concurrent objects", 2010). A module's proof uses only the models of the modules it builds on, so swapping an implementation for another that refines the same model keeps every proof above it. Each implementation level (composed Eff, machine-native, C) is a bank entry refining one model. (2) **A module's instance and a service are one thing** (representation): the module form (row 331 (6)) generates a record of closures over the module's cell (row 381 (3)). A Queue, a Stream and an application's service are each such a record, made by a constructor or provided by a layer, with one claim kind and one Effect face. The machine provides only primitive effects (`Ref`, `Deferred`, scopes, fibers, the clock, host rows); every Effect module is composed in the language a user writes. (3) **The order** (coordinator): a refinement order on the meaning and its congruence theorem (the order is not `Effects.Program.Approx`, which is budget approximation); then the first composition law, G10, SynchronizedRef's claim from Semaphore's claim alone; then the module form generating the definitions, the record, the Effect face and the owed refinement statements; then the Queue, Semaphore, Pool and Latch ported onto it | rows 330, 331, 381; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-14-one-fiber-scout.md`; `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-14-many-fibers-scout.md` | owner | **ruled 2026-10-14**, in session, by option cards (every recommendation taken); (3) is the coordinator's; (1) and (3) paused by row 387, (2) stands |
| 383 | The coordinator's decisions of 2026-10-14, under the owner's delegation: the procedures close-out re-scoped after Gemini's review | The owner, 2026-10-14, on the close-out plan: every item is wanted and its users are known; a statement that falls short of them widens the plan, never drops the item. Gemini's review (`git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-14-proc-close/gemini-review.md`, its probe re-run by the coordinator) and the coordinator's corrections (`git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-14-proc-close/review-notes.md`) give six rulings, each on semantics. (1) **One refinement order, built once on the meaning** (meaning): the order is inclusion of completed observations (the exit, the stores and the host state). It is defined on budget-indexed families of meaning trees as the limit of `Effects.Program.Approx` (every budget of the left is approximated at some budget of the right), so it is not `Approx` itself (row 382 (3)). On programs it is the pullback through the block meaning (`RefinesB`), with equivalence as refinement both ways. Its congruence is proved once per constructor, from `cata_blockAlgB_approx` on the block fragment. The inlining law G7 and row 382 (1)'s module claims are statements in this one order, and a module's model stands on its right side as a handler. Each widening of the meaning owes the congruence cases of its constructors: SCOPE-1 for masks, interruption, scopes, finalizers and layers; CONC-1 for forks, awaits, deferred awaits and races, with inclusion over decision tapes. (2) **G7 in the convergence form, by a budget sandwich** (meaning): an invocation and its inlining agree at no single budget (the review's probe: two controls). The inlining at budget `k` approximates the invocation at `k + 1`, which approximates the inlining at `k + 1`. So the step law is that two-sided approximation, and G7 states equal completed observations at some budget, in every context. (3) **The definition connectors are their own claims** (domain): `queue-definitions-agree` and `semaphore-definitions-agree` relate a client over the definition form (`Defs.lean`) to the same client over the expansion (`Ops.lean`). They are distinct from `queue-expansion-agrees` and `semaphore-expansion-agrees`, which relate the expansion to the model, and compose with them by transitivity of (1). The probe finds only `Queue.size` and `Semaphore.takeIfAvailable` inside the block fragment; the other operations reach the connectors through SCOPE-1's and CONC-1's congruence cases, whose stages carry them. (4) **CX3 is typing preservation of inlining at the checker** (representation): the shift is the existing generated `Eff.weaken` with `check_weaken`, and the one new traversal is parameter substitution, written as a fold. The typed run keeps its path-addressed points (the CX note §3.4); CX3 types the inlined program, not a point inside it. CX4 is proved already (`call_arm`, `invoke_arm` and `param_arm` with `hop_typed`, `src/Effect4/Laws/Program/Typed/Denotation.lean`). (5) **One stage for closures held as data, one activation stack and SCOPE-1's stage 0** (representation): PROC-8's value form, the merge of `Point.params` and `Point.call`, and SCOPE-1's stage 0 edit the same signatures and `Point`. The close-out's design note designs them as one stage, and it lands as SCOPE-1's stage 0. (6) **The order of the line** (domain): PROC-7 (generic definitions) and HO-5 (methods with program parameters, `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-09-program-parameters.md`) come before the module form. | 384 | The coordinator's decisions of 2026-10-14, under the owner's delegation: the modelling check of the verified line | The owner, 2026-10-14: "let's just make sure we're solid on our basic mathematical modeling... we might even find there's some area for improvement". `git:98a29895b800556365a2edc9924f3ed12787b820:docs/research/2026-10-14-proc-close/modelling-check.md` writes out every relation, from a module's model to rc.112. (2) **The call handler is the plug point** (representation): the block meaning takes its call handler as an argument, and a module's model is a handler in the implementation's place. Hosts, the scheduler and module models are all comodels, and an implementation is a handler (`Comodel.through`). (3) **Encapsulation by construction** (representation): today a Queue's handle is its cell's `Ref`, so a client can break any model claim. The module form's closures are opaque: no projection of captured values, no equality, no codec, and the printer writes the type only. Until the ports, a module claim states the premise that the client uses the handle only through the module's operations, decided by a located refusal. (4) **Names compare up to renaming in module claims** (meaning): exact `obs` stays for G7 and determinism (row 361 (2)); DET-OW-05's comparison becomes a requirement of the bank. (5) **A bank claim has two parts** (domain): refinement, which is safety, and refinement that keeps termination under fair tapes, which is progress (R12). (6) **The many-fiber claim is linearizability of a two-phase object** (meaning): the tree's models keep waiters in arrival order, so a waiting request is linearized at its enrolment and at its fulfilment. (7) **Generic theorems, proved once** (domain): one object specification and one step agreement over it, with the five modules' `StepsAgree` as instances; one wrapper law in CONC-1, by reduction; one `definitions-agree` over acyclic blocks, replacing row 383 (3)'s two claims, which become its instances; and the lift law of (2). A module author then proves only sequential step agreement. (8) **The trust chain is the claim record's premises** (domain): `ClaimRecord.assumes` lists the meaning against rc.112, H9's receipt conditions, the read-back, the OCaml lanes and the host's progress, each with its grade. (9) **The proof closure** (domain): the line is done when three statements are proved and rest on no goal. K1 is the generic object theorem; K2 is the Queue's bank claim, K1 at `queue_steps_agree`; K3 is the queue-workers crew, its claims proved again from the Queue's model alone. (1) **The simulation gains a relation on results, in the `effects` package** (representation): `Step.Lift` relates finished steps at one equal value, so `IsSim` cannot relate an implementation's stores to a model's state. `Step.Lift` and `IsSim` take a relation on results; today's forms are its equality instances, with their laws restated once. The operation relation may depend on the related states (the world), answers are transported, and composition composes every relation. Three strategies serve the five modules: attempt only (Ref), the two Deferred wrappers (Queue, Semaphore, Pool) and batching (Latch). (8) **The wrapper law needs a request protocol** (meaning): registration and completion on one Deferred do not commute. The protocol tracks pending requests, committed answers, stale hints, posted helpers and withdrawals. Reduction applies only where disjointness is proved. `protectedBy` gets its masked-region law. (9) **Progress needs a fair-execution definition** (domain): a parked machine satisfies `FairTape` (`parked_empty_tape_fair`), so per-request and system progress are stated over every admitted fair execution, connected to `FairTape`, with named environment premises. (10) **Prefix observations** (meaning): the crew's claims hold over funded prefixes and at rest, so K1 carries the maintained relation at prefixes, and `H9Fibers` is stated on prefixes. Reuse `RowsEnd`, `runRowsO` and `observeRows`. (11) **A checked bank constructor** (representation): `Bank.Entry` accepts an invented digest, model or theorem name (`uncheckedEntry`). Option B's proved first step stays outside the tree (`item3b-option-b-normal-form.lean.txt`, beside the receipt). ## The order (milestone before the MCP server; state/refinement review before the concrete ledger pin)

**History, written 2026-09-19 and kept as the dated record.** Each row's status column above is the
owner of its status; a row named open here may have been ruled or landed since (rows 20, 21 and 86
are ruled; row 39 landed on 2026-10-01; rows 134–149 came from the formal pass).

The owner paused implementation on 2026-09-19 to reconcile the completed research. The proposed
D0–D7 sequence in the refinement plan refines the milestone work below; rows 78–83 are still
open. Generic language prerequisites already authorized by rows 42–43 precede the proof arms
that depend on them. Fast-container and all-backend implementations are not new prerequisites
for the typed-state milestone.

1. **Now, no rulings needed** — the small rows verified open above: 24, 16 (`Await`), 17, 18
   with 23, 5 restated at `Ty`. (Row 40 closed and row 34 ruled out on 2026-09-18: the fold work
   stops here; the thirteen exemptions are tracked, not converted.)
2. **Row 39, one ruling** — the Schema wipe. It closes 4 and 9, takes `Image` out of row 10's
   count, and ends with `render` leaving `Shape.lean`. (Landed 2026-10-01: the move was deletion
   only, so row 8's `ShapeDoc.document` fix did not land in it and stays open in
   `Schema/OfShape.lean`.) Then row 10 (one `Val → Json`) over what remains.
3. **Row 41, the milestone** — the typed-state invariant on the reference machine, in the corrected order
   of `docs/research/history/post-phase-c-synthesis.md` §§5–6: relational interfaces and valid-world
   transport, protocols before predicate assembly, stack/delivery, denotation and initialization,
   transition preservation, then transfer through `BMeans` to the compiled machine's exits.
   Direct proofs and an explicit inventory suffice while the semantic structure is changing;
   new gates are added only when they remove demonstrated repeated work.
4. **Group D** (rows 26, 27, 28, 31, 32, 29; row 30's `compileEff` ruling when row 27's root order
   reaches it): the TypeScript rules, the recipe, rule coverage, the rung-3 reader, the vendoring
   order, then the profile/legalization/IR refactor with the TypeScript printer as its first
   customer. The visual-plane research (the owner's layout and design-system documents, the
   2026-09-17 plan and its two reviews) is scouted against the printer's substrate before this
   group starts, so the TypeScript printer and the visual plane target one document algebra or
   knowingly two.
5. **Group B** (rows 14, 15, with 18 and 23): the digest, the Lean MCP driver over the `ToolSpec`
   table; then 2, 7, 11 — names at the boundary, handles, the reviver table; then the ledger and
   the pool dogfoods. Moved after group D on the owner's steer of 2026-09-18: the server would
   freeze a wire shape that LCNF handling and the milestone still change.
6. **Group C and the rest** (rows 1 with 3, 19, 20, 21, 22): the AST as second carrier when row 32
   step 2 reads a foreign schema, `Ty.app` with it, the file moves and daemon words, the machine
   edit, the two confirmations.

Owner rows open after the review (as of 2026-09-19): 1, 2, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15, 19, 20, 21, 22, 26, 27, 28, 29, 30
(`compileEff` only), 32, and the proposed policy rows 78–85; 4 and 9 are folded into 39, which is ruled.
Row 41 is ruled but unfinished. Coordinator design questions 86–88 are open technical
contracts, not newly required owner approvals. Rows 44–45 are approved; the current implementation home of Protocol is established, with its
future upstream publication still separate (row 53).
